1	# RL Lab — Architecture
2	
3	**Status:** Approved · **Shape:** Single package · **Last updated:** 2026-10-17 · **Spec:** [spec.md](spec.md)
4	
5	Single package (`rllab`): one learner, one laptop, one install, nothing deployed. The one seam that could become its own package is `refcheck`, the only component that carries Stable-Baselines3 and Gymnasium. It sits behind an optional `check` extra, so a split would move it out as it is.
6	
7	## Overview
8	Every world implements one multi-agent `World` protocol (AD-04): it resets from a seed, steps on one action array for all agents, and reports per-team scores and a drawable state. `train` drives a hand-written learner per team (AD-03) over a world for a fixed step budget. It plays held-out evaluation episodes through `core.play_episode` and writes everything to a run folder through `runlog` (AD-06). `evaluate` replays final checkpoints and both baselines on the final seeds and runs the paired permutation test. `charts` draws learning curves from run folders. `replay` re-simulates any checkpoint or baseline from a seed into a `Trace`, then shows it in a pygame window or writes it to MP4 (AD-07). `refcheck` trains Stable-Baselines3 on the same world through an adapter, kept away from every training run (AD-08).
9	
10	```mermaid
11	flowchart LR
12	  cli --> train & evaluate & charts & replay & refcheck & notes
13	  train --> methods & runlog & worlds & baselines
14	  evaluate --> methods & runlog & worlds & baselines
15	  replay --> methods & runlog & worlds & baselines
16	  refcheck --> evaluate & train & worlds
17	  charts --> runlog & evaluate
18	  methods --> runlog
19	  worlds & baselines & methods & runlog --> core
20	```
21	
22	## Tech stack
23	| Area | Choice | Decision |
24	|---|---|---|
25	| Language, env | Python 3.12, uv, one `pyproject.toml` | AD-01 |
26	| Networks, autograd | PyTorch ≥ 2.5, CPU wheels; `nn` layers and `optim` only | AD-02 |
27	| Numerics, world physics | NumPy ≥ 2.1, vectorised over agents | AD-04 |
28	| Learning methods | Hand-written DQN (discrete) and PPO (continuous), one learner per team | AD-03 |
29	| Seeds, parallel runs | `np.random.SeedSequence`, one spawned process per run | AD-05 |
30	| Run storage | JSON record + JSON Lines logs + `torch.save` checkpoints per run folder | AD-06 |
31	| Replay window, video | pygame-ce ≥ 2.5; imageio ≥ 2.36 with imageio-ffmpeg ≥ 0.5 (bundled ffmpeg) | AD-07 |
32	| Charts | matplotlib ≥ 3.9, PNG + SVG | — |
33	| Reference check | stable-baselines3 ≥ 2.4 + gymnasium ≥ 1.0, optional extra `check` | AD-08 |
34	| Settings | TOML files in `configs/`, read with `tomllib` into frozen dataclasses | — |
35	| CLI | `argparse`, one `rllab` console script | — |
36	| Tests, import rules | pytest; import-linter | — |
37	
38	## Components
39	| Component | Responsibility | Covers | Depends on |
40	|---|---|---|---|
41	| `core` | World and policy protocols, shared types, held-out seed sets, and the one episode loop every other component plays through | WORLD-01–WORLD-07 (interface), WORLD edge cases | — |
42	| `worlds` | Snake, the three fish-tank variants, predator-prey and the two check worlds, behind `worlds.make` | SNAKE-01–SNAKE-06, FISH-01–FISH-08, PREY-01–PREY-06, TRAIN-06 (check worlds) | `core` |
43	| `baselines` | Random play for any action format, each world's hand-written rule acting on observations only, and their mean scores on the evaluation seeds | EVAL-01, EVAL-02, SNAKE-07, FISH-09, PREY-07 | `core`, `worlds` |
44	| `methods` | Hand-written DQN and PPO learners behind `Learner`; rebuilds a frozen `Policy` from a checkpoint | CHECK-01, TRAIN-07 | `core`, `runlog` |
45	| `train` | One run over the step budget: per-team learners, checkpoints, in-training evaluations, invalid-number stop; batches across cores; the check-world gate | TRAIN-01–TRAIN-07, QR-01–QR-03, TRAIN edge cases | `core`, `worlds`, `baselines`, `methods`, `runlog` |
46	| `runlog` | Run folders: record, episode and evaluation logs, checkpoints, live progress, run lookup | LOG-01, LOG-02, LOG-06, TRAIN-03 (storage), TRAIN edge cases (overwrite, incomplete) | `core` |
47	| `charts` | Learning curves with range bands and baseline lines, extra measures, two-team curves, pairing heatmaps; follows the `dataviz` skill | LOG-03–LOG-05, PREY-10, PREY-09 (chart), LOG edge cases | `runlog`, `evaluate` |
48	| `evaluate` | Final evaluation on the final seeds, paired permutation tests run with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill, verdicts, summaries, fixed-opponent team evaluation, pairing grids, collapse flags | EVAL-03–EVAL-06, SNAKE-08, FISH-10, PREY-08, PREY-09, QR-05, EVAL and PREY edge cases | `core`, `worlds`, `baselines`, `methods`, `runlog` |
49	| `replay` | Opens replay sides, re-simulates checkpoints or baselines into a `Trace`; pygame viewer with pause, step, speeds, side by side and sight overlay, testable headless; MP4 export | REPLAY-01–REPLAY-08, QR-04, REPLAY edge cases | `core`, `worlds`, `baselines`, `methods`, `runlog` |
50	| `refcheck` | Trains and evaluates Stable-Baselines3 DQN/PPO on a world through a Gymnasium/VecEnv adapter, and compares with the hand-written summary | CHECK-02, CHECK-03, CHECK edge case | `core`, `worlds`, `train`, `evaluate`, `runlog` |
51	| `notes` | Write-up scaffold with the three headings only, and the finish check (parts present, struggle link, broken links) | NOTES-01–NOTES-03, NOTES edge case | — |
52	| `cli` | The `rllab` command: dispatches each trigger to its pipeline entry and prints the result; owns no step (AD-10) | LOG-06 (view), REPLAY-01 (entry) | `core`, `worlds`, `baselines`, `methods`, `train`, `runlog`, `charts`, `evaluate`, `replay`, `refcheck`, `notes` |
53	
54	## Interfaces
55	See [packages/rllab/contract.md](packages/rllab/contract.md).
56	
57	## Data
58	No external datasets: every world is simulated. Generated data lives under `runs/<world>/<variant>/<run-name>/` (AD-06):
59	- `run.json`: the LOG-01 record, the run's config and its status (`running`, `finished`, `incomplete`, `failed` with step and value).
60	- `episodes.jsonl`: one line per training episode (LOG-02). `evals.jsonl`: one line per 5% evaluation point (TRAIN-04), flushed as written so `rllab watch` reads it live (LOG-06).
61	- `checkpoints/ckpt_<pct>.pt`: 11 checkpoints, every 10% (TRAIN-03), each holding the `WorldSpec`, per-team method configs and state dicts.
62	- Final evaluations under `runs/<world>/<variant>/_eval/<date>/` (`summary.json`, per-episode scores), reference runs under `_reference/`, charts and videos under `reports/<world>/`, write-ups at `notes/<world>.md`.
63	
64	Seeds come from three disjoint ranges (AD-05): training episode seeds drawn by the run's generator from [0, 10⁶), the 20 in-training evaluation seeds 1,000,000–1,000,019, and the 100 final seeds 2,000,000–2,000,099, shared by every run and baseline.
65	
66	## Cross-cutting concerns
67	- **Determinism (WORLD-02, TRAIN-02, REPLAY-02, FISH-11, PREY-11):** no global random state. Each episode seed spawns the world's and the policies' generators, and torch runs single-threaded with deterministic algorithms (AD-05). A committed digest of a short snake run's logs and final checkpoint is checked in CI. A later world that changes it fails the build (FISH-11, PREY-11), and so does a fish digest once S2 lands.
68	- **No outside RL code in training (CHECK-01):** import-linter forbids `stable_baselines3` and `gymnasium` outside `refcheck`, and a test asserts neither is in `sys.modules` after a training run (AD-08).
69	- **Speed (QR-01, QR-02, QR-04, QR-05):** world physics is NumPy over all agents at once, learners batch every team's observations into one forward pass, and evaluation spreads seeds over processes. `rllab` logs each run's wall time against the 60-minute budget, and a timing test plays a 1,000-step replay headless.
70	- **GPU (QR-03):** `device` is a run setting, `cpu` by default; a rented GPU run uses the same code, and its cost goes in the write-up by hand.
71	- **Offline (QR-06):** nothing imports a network client. ffmpeg comes bundled with imageio-ffmpeg, and fonts come from pygame's built-in one.
72	- **Outputs:** every step that writes a chart, video or write-up returns a `core.Output` naming the file and what it was made from, so a write-up can cite it (NOTES-02).
73	- **Errors:** bad actions raise `core.ActionError` naming the agent and value (never clipped). Spec mismatches on checkpoint load raise `methods.SpecMismatchError` naming the field. Mixed-settings charts raise `charts.MixedRunsError` naming the differing settings.
74	- **Skills:** `charts` follows `dataviz` for every chart (LOG-04). `evaluate.compare` is built and checked with `null-hypothesis-testing`, whose inputs are its signature (EVAL-04).
75	
76	## Project layout
77	```
78	src/rllab/     → core/ worlds/ baselines/ methods/ train/ runlog/ charts/ evaluate/ replay/ refcheck/ notes/ cli/ — one folder per component; __init__ exports its contract
79	                 train/pipelines/ holds the PL-01–PL-03 entries, cli/pipelines/ the PL-04–PL-12 entries (AD-10)
80	configs/       → one TOML per world and variant: budget, per-team method settings
81	runs/          → generated run folders (git-ignored)
82	reports/       → charts and videos kept alongside the write-ups
83	notes/         → one write-up per world, the learner's own words
84	tests/         → folders only; files are planned in pipelines.md
85	```
86	
87	## Decisions
88	### AD-01: Python 3.12 in one uv-managed package
89	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
90	- **Context:** One learner on an Apple Silicon laptop; PyTorch, pygame-ce and Stable-Baselines3 all ship arm64 macOS wheels for Python. QR-06 needs the whole install to work offline once set up.
91	- **Decision:** Python 3.12, one `rllab` package with a `uv.lock`. Stable-Baselines3 and Gymnasium go in an optional `check` extra.
92	- **Alternatives:** Python 3.13: wheels for some libraries lag. Separate `rllab-check` package: a real boundary only if the reference stack becomes a burden, so it is a named seam for now.
93	- **Consequences:** `requires-python = ">=3.12,<3.13"`. Training can run without the `check` extra installed, which proves CHECK-01 at install level too.
94	
95	### AD-02: PyTorch for networks and gradients, not for the learning algorithm
96	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
97	- **Context:** CHECK-01 allows automatic gradient computation but the learner writes action choice, learning targets and updates. Networks are small MLPs on observations of tens of numbers (WORLD-03). Training is CPU-only (QR-01).
98	- **Decision:** Learners use `torch.nn` layers, `torch.optim` optimisers and autograd. Replay buffers, exploration, targets, advantage estimation, clipping and update schedules are hand-written in `methods`.
99	- **Alternatives:** NumPy with hand-derived backprop: teaches backprop, which is not this project's subject, and is slower to debug. JAX: capable, but less familiar CPU tooling and a functional style that hides the update loop.
100	- **Consequences:** `torch.set_num_threads(1)` per run process (AD-05); small batches stay fast on CPU. The PyTorch install is about 200 MB.
101	
102	### AD-03: DQN for discrete actions, PPO for continuous ones, one learner per team
103	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
104	- **Context:** Snake has 3 discrete actions (SNAKE-02); fish and predator-prey push with two numbers in [−1, 1] (FISH-02, PREY-02). Many-fish has 8 agents (FISH-06), and predator-prey has two teams learning at once (TRAIN-07). The brief wants each world to teach one idea, and wants checking against a reference implementation (CHECK-02).
105	- **Decision:** `dqn` (replay buffer, target network, ε-greedy, Huber loss) for snake and the discrete check world. `ppo` (Gaussian policy, GAE, clipped objective) for fish, predator-prey and the continuous check world. All agents of a team share one learner (parameter sharing over a batch of agents). Teams have separate learners in the same run. Evaluation and replay act deterministically: greedy for DQN, the mean action for PPO.
106	- **Alternatives:** PPO everywhere: one method fewer, but snake would not teach value learning. SAC for fish: strong on continuous control, but a third method and harder to explain for many agents. Tabular Q-learning for snake: SNAKE-06 includes length, an unbounded number, so the table would need discretising.
107	- **Consequences:** Two methods to write, each with a check world (TRAIN-06) and a Stable-Baselines3 equivalent (AD-08). A prey that leaves the episode is masked out of its team's batch.
108	
109	### AD-04: Our own multi-agent World protocol, not Gymnasium or PettingZoo
110	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
111	- **Context:** WORLD-01–WORLD-07 need seeded resets, a declared spec, team-labelled agents, per-team scores with a direction, and a drawable state. The brief bets that one core serves a grid game, smooth movement and two teams.
112	- **Decision:** `core.World` is a small protocol. Observations, actions, rewards and active masks are NumPy arrays indexed by agent, teams map to agent indices in `WorldSpec`, and `draw()` returns a `DrawState`. "Not seen" entries are a 0/1 flag followed by zeros, so lengths stay fixed (WORLD-03, FISH-07, PREY-06).
113	- **Alternatives:** Gymnasium: single-agent, with no teams, score direction or drawable state, so all of those would be extras. PettingZoo parallel API: multi-agent, but dict-per-agent stepping is slow in Python and adds a dependency the learner didn't write.
114	- **Consequences:** `refcheck` writes the one Gymnasium/VecEnv adapter (AD-08). Every world implements all five methods, and `core.play_episode` is the only episode loop.
115	
116	### AD-05: Seeds from SeedSequence, disjoint held-out ranges, one process per run
117	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
118	- **Context:** WORLD-01, WORLD-02, TRAIN-02 and REPLAY-02 need exact repeats; TRAIN-04 and EVAL-03 need seeds never used in training; TRAIN-05 and QR-02 need runs in parallel, one per core.
119	- **Decision:** A run seed spawns the learner's, exploration and episode-seed generators through `np.random.SeedSequence`. An episode seed spawns the world's and the policies' generators inside `core.play_episode`. Training draws episode seeds from [0, 10⁶); evaluation seeds are the fixed `core.EVAL_SEEDS` and `core.FINAL_SEEDS` ranges (see Data). `train.run_batch` runs each config in its own `spawn` process with `torch.set_num_threads(1)` and `torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(True)`.
120	- **Alternatives:** Global `np.random.seed`/`torch.manual_seed`: order-dependent, breaks once evaluation interleaves with training. Threads: the GIL serialises the Python world loop.
121	- **Consequences:** Identical runs are promised on the same machine only (TRAIN-02). In-training evaluation never touches the training generators, so turning evaluation off for a test leaves training identical.
122	
123	### AD-06: Run folders of JSON, JSON Lines and torch checkpoints
124	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
125	- **Context:** LOG-01 and LOG-02 need a record and per-episode logs. LOG-06 needs them readable while the run writes, at most one evaluation point behind. A stopped run keeps its checkpoints and is marked incomplete. A run name that already exists is refused (TRAIN edge cases). REPLAY edge cases need a checkpoint to carry its world spec.
126	- **Decision:** One folder per run (see Data). Logs are append-only JSON Lines, flushed at every evaluation point; `run.json` is rewritten atomically (write, then rename) on each status change. A checkpoint is a `torch.save` dict of spec, method configs and state dicts, loaded with `weights_only=True`.
127	- **Alternatives:** Parquet: fast to read but not appendable while a run is live. SQLite: one file for everything, but concurrent writers from parallel runs need care and the files are no longer readable by eye.
128	- **Consequences:** Charts and evaluation read whole JSONL files; at ~10⁴ episodes per run that is instant. A crashed process leaves `run.json` at `running`; `runlog` reports any `running` run with no live process as incomplete.
129	
130	### AD-07: Replays re-simulate into a Trace, shown with pygame-ce and saved with imageio-ffmpeg
131	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
132	- **Context:** REPLAY-01–REPLAY-08 need any checkpoint or baseline from any seed, step back, 4 speeds, side by side, a sight overlay and video export. QR-04 needs ≥ 30 steps/s; QR-06 needs it offline. Worlds are deterministic (AD-05), so nothing needs recording during training.
133	- **Decision:** `replay.trace_sides` (or, for one side, `to_trace` over a traced `core.play_episode`) plays the whole episode headless through `core.play_episode` into a `Trace` of frames (at most the world's `max_steps`), then the viewer only draws frames. Stepping back is an index change. 1× is 30 steps/s. pygame-ce draws circles, cells and text; MP4 (H.264) export goes through imageio's ffmpeg writer.
134	- **Alternatives:** matplotlib animation: slow at 16× for 8 fish and clumsy keyboard control. A browser viewer: smooth, but a local web server and JavaScript stack for one user (spec: everything local).
135	- **Consequences:** The `Trace` is testable without a window, so REPLAY-02 is a frame comparison. Memory is one episode of `DrawState`s, under 1,000 frames of a few dozen objects.
136	
137	### AD-08: Stable-Baselines3 as the reference, isolated in `refcheck`
138	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
139	- **Context:** CHECK-02 needs an established implementation of the same kind of method (DQN, PPO; AD-03) trained on the same world, budget and seeds; CHECK-01 forbids it inside training runs. Worlds speak `core.World`, not Gymnasium (AD-04).
140	- **Decision:** `refcheck` wraps a world as a Gymnasium env (single agent) or as an SB3 `VecEnv` with one slot per agent of the learning team (many-fish: 8 slots stepping one world, matching parameter sharing). It trains SB3 `DQN` or `PPO` for 5 seeds and evaluates on `core.FINAL_SEEDS` through `core.play_episode`. In predator-prey, SB3 cannot co-train two models, so each team's reference trains against the rule-based opponent, the same opponent PREY-08 evaluates against.
141	- **Alternatives:** CleanRL single-file scripts: easy to read, but they would need editing to fit our worlds, which weakens them as a reference. RLlib: multi-agent native, but a heavy distributed stack for a laptop.
142	- **Consequences:** SB3's default hyperparameters differ from ours; `refcheck` starts from the hand-written method's settings where SB3 has the same knob. The predator-prey reference answers a narrower question than co-training, so CHECK-03's 80% bar compares a fixed-opponent reference with a co-trained team there; the learner accepted this (2026-10-17).
143	
144	### AD-09: Every batch first runs the method's check world
145	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
146	- **Context:** TRAIN-06 needs every learning method to learn a hand-derived best behaviour before it is used in a world. Bugs in RL are silent (brief), and methods change while a world is built.
147	- **Decision:** `train.run_check` trains each team method in a config on its check world: for DQN a 6-state corridor with two exits, left of state 0 (reward 0.8) and right of state 5 (reward 1.0), γ = 0.9, so the best action is left in states 0–1 and right in 2–5 and a learner biased to one action can't pass; for PPO a 1-D point that is rewarded for reaching a target, where the best push is the sign of the offset. It needs ≥ 95% agreement over every check-world state. `train.run_batch` runs the same check steps with the batch's method settings before starting any run and refuses the batch on failure. `rllab check <config>` runs it alone.
148	- **Alternatives:** A recorded pass per method version: saves seconds but goes stale silently when code changes. A test only: passes in CI but not for the settings actually used.
149	- **Consequences:** Each batch spends under a minute on the check. A check-world settings override exists because a world's tuned settings (e.g. buffer size) may be too large for a 6-state world.
150	
151	### AD-10: Pipeline entries are the public API; `cli` only dispatches
152	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
153	- **Context:** Pipeline design gave every workflow one entry function calling named steps (PL-01–PL-12). Entries import their steps' owners, so where an entry lives sets an import direction. PL-03 runs PL-02 in worker processes, so `train` can't depend on `cli`. A chart, a summary and a heatmap each need two or more components that the `cli` row already depends on.
154	- **Decision:** `train` hosts the PL-01–PL-03 entries (`run_check`, `run_train`, `run_batch`) and exports them as its contract. Every other entry lives in `cli/pipelines/`. Entries return contract types. `cli/__main__.py` parses arguments, loads configs through `train.load_run_configs`, calls the entry, prints its result and sets the exit code; `cli` owns no pipeline step.
155	- **Alternatives:** All entries in `cli`: the batch's worker processes would import `cli`, inverting the dependency. Each entry in its main component: `charts` and `evaluate` entries would need `runlog` and `baselines` steps, which they may do, but the replay and refcheck entries would need printing in their own components.
156	- **Consequences:** The contract carries the train entries, the config loader and every step function a `cli` entry calls. `cli` has no step tests; its intent tests drive `main` with arguments.
157	
158	### AD-11: Stay a single package past the size triggers
159	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
160	- **Context:** The contract passed its 200-line size ceiling and the package has 12 pipelines, past the ~3-pipeline Grow trigger. The learner chose to stay single (2026-10-17). Nothing runs or deploys on its own, and the one dependency stack the rest shouldn't carry (Stable-Baselines3, Gymnasium) is already isolated as the optional `check` extra in `refcheck` (AD-08).
161	- **Decision:** `rllab` stays one package. Size is handled by `dt context`, which loads only the contract sections and pipelines a task reads, and import-linter keeps the component directions.
162	- **Alternatives:** Split `refcheck` into its own package: a real boundary, but already met by the extra. Split the shared core from the worlds: every world needs the core, and the core has no other consumer.
163	- **Consequences:** The Grow triggers stay reported by `dt` and are answered by this decision. A new trigger (a part that runs on its own, a heavy new stack) reopens it.
164	
165	## Risks and open questions
166	- **Many-fish and predator-prey speed (QR-01, QR-02).** 8–10 agents, 1,000-step episodes and 21 × 20 evaluation episodes may not fit 60 minutes in Python. Mitigation: NumPy physics over all agents, evaluation in a worker process, and a timing check in the first fish build; QR-03's GPU route doesn't help a CPU-bound world loop.
167	- **5 parallel runs on mixed cores (QR-02).** Apple Silicon's efficiency cores run slower; 5 runs may not all land on performance cores. Measured in the first snake batch.
168

1	# Milestone shape: interface, step spines, wiring, system
2	
3	Read when writing a milestone's tasks. Examples: `examples-phase0.md`.
4	
5	A milestone is planned from its pipelines' steps: each step has one owner, one module and typed `In → Out`, so it is the unit of work. The plan freezes every boundary between steps first, so the work on both sides starts at once instead of queueing behind one spine. Every task carries `**Kind:**`:
6	
7	```
8	## Phase 0: Interface              interface (solo, first)       types, signatures, samples, fakes, exports, __main__
9	## Phase 1: Step spines            spine, one per step           each writes its owner module only
10	## Phase 2: Wiring and thicken     wiring, one per pipeline      its entry module, smoke test
11	                                   thicken (tasks.md)            each after its step's spine
12	## Phase 3: System                 system (solo, last)           fakes swapped for real providers
13	```
14	
15	Phases name the usual waves; the dependency graph (`tasks.md` step 9) decides what may start. Tester halves of split tasks start right after the interface.
16	
17	**a. Interface task** (`**Kind:** interface`, `**Step:** contract`, solo, `**Depends on:** —`). It freezes, as code, every boundary in the milestone whose two sides belong to different tasks:
18	- each contract `type` section the milestone's steps pass (`In → Out`), written into its owner's `types.py`, with a schema (pandera for a frame, JSON Schema across runtimes) and one sample per case in `tests/contracts/<component>.<Type>/`. A case naming a probe sample (`PB-01 samples/sync_added.json#0`) puts `PB-01:samples` in `Read:`; the task derives the type's sample from it, never copies it
19	- each new step's signature in its owner module, with the body `raise NotImplementedError("<task id>")` naming the task that replaces it
20	- for each function a pipeline calls from another pipeline or package (its `**Boundary:**` line): a fixture-backed fake serving its fixtures, where the caller's tests load it. A `call` edge to a contract symbol is mitigated by that fake; `none` there needs a reason (`dt lint`)
21	- for each object with behavior a step passes (a Protocol or class: `core.Dataset`, `runstore.RunWriter`) and each call into another component's function: the Protocol or signature plus a fixture-backed replay double in `tests/contracts/<component>.<Type>/`. The double is that type's sample, not a fake: the system task never swaps it
22	- each component's `__init__.py` exports, and the package's `__main__.py`: the dispatch from each pipeline's trigger to its entry function, whose signature it stubs naming the wiring task, in that pipeline's own entry module
23	
24	Not frozen: helpers, private functions, anything one task owns end to end, and boundaries an earlier milestone froze. `Read:` the type and boundary symbols. Acceptance: every sample validates against its type; every signature imports; each fake and replay double serves its fixtures. `Writes:` each component folder as one glob (`src/gridload/profile/**`) plus its contract folders; `task-size` doesn't count them. A milestone across packages may have one interface task per provider, all solo and first; interface tasks never depend on each other. When one runtime's types are generated from another's schema, the providing interface task runs the generator and writes the generated file too (its `Writes:` claims it).
25	
26	**b. Step spines** (`**Kind:** spine`, `**Step:** PL-NN.<function>`, solo, XS or S). One per new step, each depending only on the interface task: it replaces the stub naming it with the step's spine version from the step table. `Writes:` its owner module and that module's unit test, nothing else; steps sharing one module share one spine task. Acceptance: the step's input sample gives a schema-valid output; the signature is unchanged. A step another pipeline provides (`PL-01 as built`) gets no spine.
27	
28	**c. Wiring** (`**Kind:** wiring`, `**Step:** PL-NN`, solo). One per new pipeline: the entry function in the pipeline's own entry module (the layout's, never one module shared by several pipelines), so wiring tasks run side by side, and its smoke test. `__main__.py` is the interface task's. It depends on its pipeline's spines, since the smoke runs them (`order`, `none: integration: …`), never on their thicken tasks. A call to another pipeline or package runs against that boundary's fake, so wiring PL-02 never waits for PL-01. Acceptance: the smoke command runs end to end and is committed as the pipeline's smoke test; the pipeline's pre-build sections are replaced by a `dt layout` pointer (`dt land` runs `dt prebuild-clear` when the wiring lands).
29	
30	**d. System task** (`**Kind:** system`, solo, last). It swaps each fake for the real provider, in the entry its wiring wrote (a `file` edge), and runs every smoke command and the milestone's golden cases on the real chain; the boundary samples still validate against real output. Multi-package: one per new system pipeline, `**Step:**` that pipeline, `**Package:**` the owner of its first step, `**Reason:** system task`; the root has no pre-build sections, so no `dt layout` bullet. Single package: `**Step:**` the calling pipeline. A milestone with no fake has no system task.
31	
32	**Folds** keep the task count down. The pipeline's wiring depends on a fold task, so a fold is allowed only where that task is XS and the critical path doesn't grow. Record each on its task as `**Reason:** fold: <rule>`:
33	- A step with a golden case whose spine and thicken are both in the milestone, or whose spine version is its whole behavior, may be one split thicken task that replaces the stub itself; with a thicken folded in, its acceptance keeps the baseline selectable.
34	- A trivial step (pass-through, print) is built by its pipeline's wiring task; its stub names the wiring task.
35	
36	**Fakes and stubs.** A fake is allowed only where a pipeline calls another pipeline or package: both sides test against the same fixtures, so neither drifts from the contract unseen. Mocking a step is forbidden: a step's spine is the real baseline, and its consumer builds against the type's sample (for a Protocol or class, its replay double). The interface stubs are the floor's one stub carve-out (`build-setup`'s CR-02): allowed while the task they name is open; the gate fails on one naming a done task, the milestone exit on any.
37	
38	**Frozen means frozen.** Changing a frozen type, sample, signature or fake is a `contract-change`: `architecture-design` Revise, then a new interface task naming the symbol in `Read:`, never an edit to make a test pass. The bar enforces it (CR-09): once a contract folder exists at a task's base the implementer can't write it, and `dt guard` asks before any edit no open interface task covers. The re-freeze is that interface task, run by an implementer agent, never the main session: its `Writes:` names the frozen folders it changes, which opens them to it alone.
39

  "file_path": "/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-all-at-once-A3-r1/with_skill/project/.claude/worktrees/agent-a8b65a52fd63d455a/scripts/hooks/stop_gate.sh"

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	# Stop hook (becomes SubagentStop inside a subagent): block finishing until the gate passes.
3	#   stop_gate.sh collect|gate|full [--warn]
4	# Blocks by printing {"decision": "block", "reason": ...} and exiting 0, not by exit 2:
5	# Claude Code treats an exit-2 Stop hook whose stderr mentions a missing file
6	# ("No such file or directory") as non-blocking, and test output often does.
7	# Loop guard: after MAX_TRIES failed attempts the agent may stop. Outside a dt build the last
8	# block tells it to start its report with "GATE FAILED", because that report is all the parent
9	# sees: a SubagentStop systemMessage doesn't reach the main view (checked live, Claude Code 2.1.292).
10	# --warn never blocks: a failing gate only leaves a systemMessage (CONSTRAINTS.md says when).
11	# For gate and full, .claude/.check_pkg (written by dt claim, optional) holds the task's package
12	# and becomes --pkg.
13	# In a dt build, .claude/.dt (written by dt claim) holds the path to dt.py: an agent whose claim
14	# has an escalation record (dt escalate) may stop without the gate, and every gate result is
15	# recorded with `dt build verdict`, which dt land reuses when the tree hasn't changed since.
16	mode=gate
17	warn=0
18	for a in "$@"; do
19	  case "$a" in
20	    --warn) warn=1 ;;
21	    *) mode="$a" ;;
22	  esac
23	done
24	MAX_TRIES=3
25	source "$(dirname "$0")/project_root.sh"
26	
27	# Without jq there is no safe way to write the JSON answer: block the plain way (exit 2,
28	# fixed message) rather than let the agent stop on a gate nobody ran.
29	say_block() {
30	  if command -v jq >/dev/null 2>&1; then
31	    jq -n --arg reason "$1" '{decision: "block", reason: $reason}'
32	    exit 0
33	  fi
34	  echo "stop_gate: the gate failed and jq is not installed to report it; install jq, then run scripts/check.sh $mode" >&2
35	  exit 2
36	}
37	say_user() {
38	  command -v jq >/dev/null 2>&1 && jq -n --arg msg "$1" '{systemMessage: $msg}'
39	  exit 0
40	}
41	
42	cd "$root" || say_block "stop_gate: cannot enter the project root $root"
43	id=$(jq -r '.agent_id // .session_id // "main"' <<<"$input" 2>/dev/null | tr -cd 'A-Za-z0-9_-')
44	counter=".claude/.stop_attempts_${id:-main}"
45	mkdir -p .claude
46	
47	dt=""
48	[ -s .claude/.dt ] && dt=$(head -n 1 .claude/.dt)
49	if [ -n "$dt" ] && python3 "$dt" build escalated >/dev/null 2>&1; then
50	  rm -f "$counter"
51	  exit 0
52	fi
53	
54	pkg_args=()
55	if [ "$mode" != collect ] && [ -s .claude/.check_pkg ]; then
56	  pkg_args=(--pkg "$(tr -cd 'A-Za-z0-9_.-' <.claude/.check_pkg)")
57	fi
58	
59	out=$(bash scripts/check.sh "$mode" ${pkg_args[@]+"${pkg_args[@]}"} 2>&1)
60	rc=$?
61	[ -n "$dt" ] && python3 "$dt" build verdict --mode "$mode" --rc "$rc" >/dev/null 2>&1
62	if [ "$rc" -eq 0 ]; then
63	  rm -f "$counter"
64	  exit 0
65	fi
66	
67	if [ "$warn" = 1 ]; then
68	  say_user "Gate '$mode' is failing (warn only, not blocking). Run scripts/check.sh $mode to see why."
69	fi
70	
71	tries=$(cat "$counter" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
72	[[ $tries =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || tries=0
73	tries=$((tries + 1))
74	echo "$tries" >"$counter"
75	if [ "$tries" -gt "$MAX_TRIES" ]; then
76	  rm -f "$counter"
77	  say_user "Gate '$mode' still failing after $MAX_TRIES attempts; the agent stopped so a human can look. Run scripts/check.sh $mode."
78	fi
79	
80	last=""
81	if [ "$tries" -eq "$MAX_TRIES" ] && [ -n "$dt" ]; then
82	  last="
83	This was the last attempt. If you can't fix it now, finish anyway with your status line:
84	the failing verdict is recorded, and dt land rejects the task with it."
85	elif [ "$tries" -eq "$MAX_TRIES" ]; then
86	  last="
87	This was the last attempt. If you can't fix it now, finish anyway and start your report with
88	\"GATE FAILED: scripts/check.sh $mode\", then list what is still failing."
89	fi
90	say_block "Gate '$mode' failed (attempt $tries/$MAX_TRIES). Fix these before finishing:
91	$(echo "$out" | tail -60)$last"
92

121	
122	    def added_lines(self, path: str) -> set[int]:
123	        return {n for n, _ in self.added.get(path, [])}
124	
125	
126	# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- the diff
127	HUNK = re.compile(r"^@@ -\d+(?:,\d+)? \+(\d+)(?:,\d+)? @@")
128	
129	
130	def _diff_path(raw: str) -> str:
131	    raw = raw.rstrip("\t")
132	    return raw[2:] if raw[:2] in {"a/", "b/"} else raw
133	
134	
135	def parse_diff(text: str) -> Diff:
136	    """Added lines (with new line numbers), removed lines and deleted files, per file."""
137	    diff = Diff()
138	    old = new = ""
139	    line_no = 0
140	    in_header = False
141	    for line in text.splitlines():
142	        if line.startswith("diff --git "):
143	            old, new, in_header = "", "", True
144	        elif in_header and line.startswith("--- "):
145	            old = _diff_path(line[4:])
146	        elif in_header and line.startswith("+++ "):
147	            new = _diff_path(line[4:])
148	            if new == "/dev/null":
149	                diff.deleted.add(old)
150	        elif match := HUNK.match(line):
151	            in_header, line_no = False, int(match[1])
152	        elif in_header:
153	            continue
154	        elif line.startswith("+"):
155	            diff.added.setdefault(new, []).append((line_no, line[1:]))
156	            line_no += 1
157	        elif line.startswith("-"):
158	            diff.removed.setdefault(old, []).append(line[1:])
159	    return diff
160	
161	
162	def add_untracked(diff: Diff, paths: list[str]) -> None:
163	    """An untracked file is all added lines."""
164	    for name in paths:
165	        path = Path(name)
166	        if not path.is_file() or path.stat().st_size > 2_000_000:
167	            continue
168	        try:
169	            text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
170	        except (UnicodeDecodeError, OSError):
171	            continue
172	        diff.added[name] = list(enumerate(text.splitlines(), start=1))
173	
174	
175	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- line checks
176	def is_outside(path: str) -> bool:
177	    return path.startswith(tuple(f"{folder}/" for folder in OUTSIDE))
178	
179	
180	def is_python(path: str) -> bool:
181	    if path.startswith("scripts/") or is_outside(path):
182	        return False
183	    return path.endswith((".py", ".pyi"))
184	
185	
186	def is_test_file(path: str) -> bool:
187	    parts = Path(path).parts
188	    name = parts[-1] if parts else ""
189	    return (
190	        "tests" in parts
191	        and (name.startswith("test_") or name.endswith("_test.py"))
192	        and not is_outside(path)
193	    )
194	
195	
196	def line_findings(diff: Diff) -> list[Finding]:
197	    found: list[Finding] = []
198	    for path, lines in diff.added.items():
199	        python, test = is_python(path), is_test_file(path)
200	        path_settings = not is_outside(path) and not path.endswith(DOC_SUFFIXES)
201	        for n, text in lines:
202	            if python and SUPPRESSION.search(text):
203	                found.append(Finding("suppression", path, n, "new suppression comment"))
204	            if path_settings and IMPORT_PATH_SETTING.search(text):
205	                found += [
206	                    Finding(f"{folder}-path", path, n, f"{folder}/ on an import path")
207	                    for folder, word in OUTSIDE.items()
208	                    if word.search(text)
209	                ]
210	            if test and SKIP.search(text):
211	                found.append(Finding("test-skipped", path, n, "test skipped or marked xfail"))
212	            if Path(path).name not in LOCKFILES:
213	                found += [
214	                    Finding("secret", path, n, f"looks like a {kind} (value not shown)")
215	                    for kind, pattern in SECRETS.items()
216	                    if pattern.search(text)
217	                ]
218	    return found
219	
220	
221	def test_file_findings(diff: Diff) -> list[Finding]:
222	    found = [
223	        Finding("test-deleted", p, 0, "test file deleted") for p in diff.deleted if is_test_file(p)
224	    ]
225	    for path, lines in diff.removed.items():
226	        if not is_test_file(path) or path in diff.deleted:
227	            continue
228	        lost = sum(bool(ASSERTION.search(t)) for t in lines)
229	        gained = sum(bool(ASSERTION.search(t)) for _, t in diff.added.get(path, []))
230	        if lost > gained:
231	            found.append(
232	                Finding("assertion-removed", path, 0, f"{lost - gained} assertion line(s) removed")
233	            )
234	    return found
235	
236	
237	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- AST checks
238	def _touches(node: ast.AST, lines: set[int]) -> bool:
239	    start = getattr(node, "lineno", 0)
240	    end = getattr(node, "end_lineno", start) or start
241	    return any(start <= n <= end for n in lines)
242	
243	
244	def _is_trivial(body: list[ast.stmt]) -> bool:
245	    """Only pass, `...` or `raise NotImplementedError`, after an optional docstring."""
246	    stmts = body[1:] if body and _is_docstring(body[0]) else body
247	    return all(_is_placeholder(s) for s in stmts)
248	
249	
250	def _task_stub(body: list[ast.stmt]) -> str | None:
251	    """The task an interface stub names: a body that is only `raise NotImplementedError("M1-T05")`."""
252	    stmts = body[1:] if body and _is_docstring(body[0]) else body
253	    if len(stmts) != 1 or not isinstance(stmts[0], ast.Raise):
254	        return None
255	    exc = stmts[0].exc
256	    if not (isinstance(exc, ast.Call) and _name(exc.func) == "NotImplementedError"):
257	        return None
258	    arg = exc.args[0] if len(exc.args) == 1 and not exc.keywords else None
259	    if (
260	        isinstance(arg, ast.Constant)
261	        and isinstance(arg.value, str)
262	        and TASK_ID.fullmatch(arg.value)
263	    ):
264	        return arg.value
265	    return None
266	
267	
268	def _is_docstring(stmt: ast.stmt) -> bool:
269	    return (
270	        isinstance(stmt, ast.Expr)
271	        and isinstance(stmt.value, ast.Constant)
272	        and isinstance(stmt.value.value, str)
273	    )
274	
275	
276	def _is_placeholder(stmt: ast.stmt) -> bool:
277	    if isinstance(stmt, ast.Pass):
278	        return True
279	    if isinstance(stmt, ast.Expr) and isinstance(stmt.value, ast.Constant):
280	        return stmt.value.value is Ellipsis
281	    if isinstance(stmt, ast.Raise) and stmt.exc is not None:
282	        exc = stmt.exc.func if isinstance(stmt.exc, ast.Call) else stmt.exc
283	        return _name(exc) == "NotImplementedError"
284	    return False
285	
286	
287	def _name(node: ast.AST) -> str | None:
288	    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
289	        return node.id
290	    if isinstance(node, ast.Attribute):
291	        return node.attr
292	    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
293	        return _name(node.func)
294	    return None
295	
296	
297	def _exempt_functions(tree: ast.Module) -> set[ast.AST]:
298	    """Abstract methods, overloads and Protocol members may have placeholder bodies."""
299	    exempt: set[ast.AST] = set()
300	    for node in ast.walk(tree):
301	        if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
302	            if {_name(d) for d in node.decorator_list} & EXEMPT_DECORATORS:
303	                exempt.add(node)
304	        elif isinstance(node, ast.ClassDef) and "Protocol" in {_name(b) for b in node.bases}:
305	            exempt.update(
306	                n for n in node.body if isinstance(n, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef))
307	            )
308	    return exempt
309	
310	
311	def _in_folder(name: str | None, folder: str) -> bool:
312	    return name is not None and (name == folder or name.startswith(f"{folder}."))
313	
314	
315	def _mentions(node: ast.AST, word: re.Pattern[str]) -> bool:
316	    return any(
317	        isinstance(n, ast.Constant) and isinstance(n.value, str) and word.search(n.value)
318	        for n in ast.walk(node)
319	    )
320	
321	
322	def _is_sys_path(node: ast.AST) -> bool:
323	    return (
324	        isinstance(node, ast.Attribute)
325	        and node.attr == "path"
326	        and isinstance(node.value, ast.Name)
327	        and node.value.id == "sys"
328	    )
329	
330	
331	def _imported_modules(node: ast.AST) -> list[str | None]:
332	    """Absolute module names an import statement or an import_module call brings in."""
333	    if isinstance(node, ast.Import):
334	        return [alias.name for alias in node.names]
335	    if isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom):
336	        return [node.module] if node.level == 0 else []
337	    if isinstance(node, ast.Call) and _name(node.func) in {"import_module", "__import__"}:
338	        first = node.args[0] if node.args else None
339	        if isinstance(first, ast.Constant) and isinstance(first.value, str):
340	            return [first.value]
341	    return []
342	
343	
344	def _changes_sys_path(node: ast.AST) -> bool:
345	    """sys.path.insert/append/extend, site.addsitedir, or an assignment to sys.path."""
346	    if isinstance(node, ast.Call) and isinstance(node.func, ast.Attribute):
347	        func = node.func
348	        return func.attr == "addsitedir" or (
349	            func.attr in SYS_PATH_CALLS and _is_sys_path(func.value)
350	        )
351	    if isinstance(node, ast.Assign):
352	        return any(_is_sys_path(target) for target in node.targets)
353	    if isinstance(node, ast.AugAssign):
354	        return _is_sys_path(node.target)
355	    return False
356	
357	
358	def _outside_finding(path: str, node: ast.AST) -> Finding | None:
359	    """CR-08: an import from references/ or probes/, or either folder added to sys.path."""
360	    line = getattr(node, "lineno", 0)
361	    for folder, word in OUTSIDE.items():
362	        if any(_in_folder(name, folder) for name in _imported_modules(node)):
363	            return Finding(f"{folder}-import", path, line, f"import from {folder}/")
364	        if _changes_sys_path(node) and _mentions(node, word):
365	            return Finding(f"{folder}-path", path, line, f"{folder}/ added to sys.path")
366	    return None
367	
368	
369	def _asserts(func: ast.AST) -> bool:
370	    for node in ast.walk(func):
371	        if isinstance(node, ast.Assert):
372	            return True
373	        name = _name(node) if isinstance(node, ast.Call) else None
374	        if name and (
375	            name.startswith("assert") or name in {"raises", "warns", "deprecated_call", "fail"}
376	        ):
377	            return True
378	    return False
379	
380	
381	def ast_findings(path: str, lines: set[int]) -> list[Finding]:
382	    try:
383	        tree = ast.parse(Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
384	    except (SyntaxError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError):
385	        return []  # ruff reports syntax errors; a deleted file has nothing to check
386	    exempt, test = _exempt_functions(tree), is_test_file(path)
387	    found: list[Finding] = []
388	    for node in ast.walk(tree):
389	        if not _touches(node, lines):
390	            continue
391	        if outside := _outside_finding(path, node):
392	            found.append(outside)
393	        if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
394	            if (
395	                node not in exempt
396	                and not path.endswith(".pyi")
397	                and _is_trivial(node.body)
398	                and not _task_stub(node.body)
399	            ):
400	                found.append(
401	                    Finding("stub", path, node.lineno, f"`{node.name}` has a placeholder body")
402	                )
403	            if test and node.name.startswith("test") and not _asserts(node):
404	                found.append(
405	                    Finding(
406	                        "test-without-assertion",
407	                        path,
408	                        node.lineno,
409	                        f"`{node.name}` asserts nothing",
410	                    )
411	                )
412	        elif isinstance(node, ast.ExceptHandler) and _is_trivial(node.body):
413	            if (None if node.type is None else _name(node.type)) in TRIVIAL_EXCEPT:
414	                found.append(
415	                    Finding("swallowed-exception", path, node.lineno, "exception swallowed")
416	                )
417	    return found
418	
419	
420	def open_tasks() -> set[str]:
421	    """Task IDs with an unchecked line in tasks/todo.md (none when there is no todo)."""
422	    path = Path("tasks/todo.md")
423	    return set(OPEN_TASK.findall(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))) if path.is_file() else set()
424	
425	
426	def task_stub_findings(paths: list[str], at_exit: bool) -> list[Finding]:
427	    """Interface stubs anywhere in the project's code: each must name an open task, and at the
428	    milestone exit none may be left. Not diff-scoped: the stub was written by an earlier task."""
429	    open_ids, found = open_tasks(), []
430	    for path in paths:
431	        if not is_python(path) or is_test_file(path) or not Path(path).is_file():
432	            continue
433	        try:
434	            tree = ast.parse(Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
435	        except (SyntaxError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError):
436	            continue
437	        for node in ast.walk(tree):
438	            if not isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
439	                continue
440	            if not (task := _task_stub(node.body)):
441	                continue
442	            if at_exit:
443	                found.append(
444	                    Finding(
445	                        "stub-left",
446	                        path,
447	                        node.lineno,
448	                        f"`{node.name}` is still the stub for {task}",
449	                    )
450	                )
451	            elif task not in open_ids:
452	                found.append(
453	                    Finding(
454	                        "stub-task",
455	                        path,
456	                        node.lineno,
457	                        f"`{node.name}` is a stub naming {task}, which is not open in tasks/todo.md",
458	                    )
459	                )
460	    return found
461	
462	
463	# ---------------------------------------------------------------- the bar: tool config
464	MAX_KEYS = (
465	    "tool.ruff.lint.mccabe.max-complexity",
466	    "tool.size_limits.max_module_lines",
467	    "tool.size_limits.max_function_lines",
468	    "tool.vulture.min_confidence",
469	    "tool.pylint.similarities.min-similarity-lines",
470	)
471	MIN_KEYS = ("tool.coverage.report.fail_under", "tool.size_limits.min_module_lines")
472	TRUE_KEYS = ("tool.mypy.strict", "tool.pytest.ini_options.xfail_strict")
473	OFF_KEYS = ("tool.pylint.main.exit-zero",)  # turning one on silences a check
474	NO_SHRINK = ("tool.ruff.lint.select", "tool.ruff.lint.extend-select")
475	NO_GROW = (
476	    "tool.ruff.lint.ignore", "tool.ruff.lint.extend-ignore", "tool.ruff.exclude",
477	    "tool.ruff.extend-exclude", "tool.ruff.lint.exclude", "tool.deptry.exclude",
478	    "tool.deptry.extend_exclude", "tool.deptry.ignore", "tool.vulture.ignore_names",
479	    "tool.vulture.exclude", "tool.mypy.exclude", "tool.pylint.main.ignore-paths",
480	    "tool.pylint.main.ignore", "tool.pylint.main.ignore-patterns",
481	)  # fmt: skip
482	# Excluding the references or probes folder is not a loosening: neither is project code (CR-08).
483	OUTSIDE_EXCLUDE = re.compile(r"\^?(references|probes)/?\*{0,2}")
484	NO_GROW_TABLES = ("tool.ruff.lint.per-file-ignores", "tool.deptry.per_rule_ignores")
485	MUST_MATCH = ("tool.importlinter", "tool.mypy.overrides", "tool.pyright")
486	STRICT_FLAGS = ("--strict-markers", "--strict-config")
487	
488	
489	def _get(data: dict[str, Any], dotted: str) -> Any:
490	    for key in dotted.split("."):
491	        if not isinstance(data, dict) or key not in data:
492	            return None
493	        data = data[key]
494	    return data
495	
496	
497	def _as_set(value: Any) -> set[str]:
498	    if isinstance(value, str):
499	        return {value}
500	    return {str(v) for v in value} if isinstance(value, list) else set()
501	
502	
503	def _limit_findings(old: dict[str, Any], new: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
504	    pylint = _get(old, "tool.ruff.lint.pylint") or {}
505	    maxes = [*MAX_KEYS, *(f"tool.ruff.lint.pylint.{k}" for k in pylint)]
506	    out = [
507	        f"{k}: {_get(old, k)} -> {_get(new, k)}"
508	        for k in maxes
509	        if _loosened(_get(old, k), _get(new, k), +1)
510	    ]
511	    out += [
512	        f"{k}: {_get(old, k)} -> {_get(new, k)}"
513	        for k in MIN_KEYS
514	        if _loosened(_get(old, k), _get(new, k), -1)
515	    ]
516	    out += [
517	        f"{k} turned off" for k in TRUE_KEYS if _get(old, k) is True and _get(new, k) is not True
518	    ]
519	    out += [f"{k} turned on" for k in OFF_KEYS if _get(new, k) is True and _get(old, k) is not True]
520	    return out
521	
522	
523	def _loosened(was: Any, now: Any, direction: int) -> bool:
524	    if not isinstance(was, (int, float)):
525	        return False
526	    if not isinstance(now, (int, float)):
527	        return True  # a limit that disappeared
528	    return (now - was) * direction > 0
529	
530	
531	def _list_findings(old: dict[str, Any], new: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
532	    out = [f"{k} lost {sorted(_as_set(_get(old, k)) - _as_set(_get(new, k)))}" for k in NO_SHRINK]
533	    for k in NO_GROW:
534	        gained = _as_set(_get(new, k)) - _as_set(_get(old, k))
535	        out.append(f"{k} gained {sorted(g for g in gained if not OUTSIDE_EXCLUDE.fullmatch(g))}")
536	    for table in NO_GROW_TABLES:
537	        was, now = _get(old, table) or {}, _get(new, table) or {}
538	        out += [
539	            f"{table}.{key} gained {sorted(_as_set(codes) - _as_set(was.get(key)))}"
540	            for key, codes in now.items()
541	            if _as_set(codes) - _as_set(was.get(key))
542	        ]
543	    flags = _as_set((_get(new, "tool.pytest.ini_options.addopts") or "").split())
544	    out += [
545	        f"pytest addopts dropped {f}"
546	        for f in STRICT_FLAGS
547	        if f in str(_get(old, "tool.pytest.ini_options.addopts")) and f not in flags
548	    ]
549	    out += [
550	        f"{k} changed (boundaries and type config change only through build-setup)"
551	        for k in MUST_MATCH
552	        if _get(old, k) != _get(new, k)
553	    ]
554	    return [o for o in out if not o.endswith("[]")]
555	
556	
557	def config_findings(old_pyproject: Path, diff: Diff) -> list[Finding]:
558	    found = [
559	        Finding("bar-loosened", p, 0, "new tool config file overrides pyproject.toml")
560	        for p in diff.added
561	        if Path(p).name in CONFIG_FILES and not p.startswith("scripts/")
562	    ]
563	    old_text = old_pyproject.read_text(encoding="utf-8") if old_pyproject.is_file() else ""
564	    if not old_text or not Path("pyproject.toml").is_file():
565	        return found
566	    try:
567	        old, new = (
568	            tomllib.loads(old_text),
569	            tomllib.loads(Path("pyproject.toml").read_text(encoding="utf-8")),
570	        )
571	    except tomllib.TOMLDecodeError as exc:
572	        return [*found, Finding("bar-loosened", "pyproject.toml", 0, f"cannot parse: {exc}")]
573	    changes = _limit_findings(old, new) + _list_findings(old, new)
574	    return found + [Finding("bar-loosened", "pyproject.toml", 0, c) for c in changes]
575	
576	
577	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------- CONSTRAINTS.md
578	RULE_REF = re.compile(r"^\s*(?:-\s*\*\*(CR-\d+)\*\*|\|\s*(CR-\d+)\s*\|)")
579	
580	
581	def _section(lines: list[str], title: str) -> tuple[int, int]:
582	    """1-based line range of a `## title` section, or (0, -1)."""
583	    start = next((i for i, t in enumerate(lines, 1) if t.strip().lower() == f"## {title}"), 0)
584	    if not start:
585	        return 0, -1
586	    end = next(
587	        (i - 1 for i, t in enumerate(lines, 1) if i > start and t.startswith("## ")), len(lines)
588	    )
589	    return start, end
590	
591	
592	def _table_rows(lines: list[str], start: int, end: int) -> list[tuple[int, list[str]]]:
593	    rows = []
594	    for n in range(start + 1, end + 1):
595	        text = lines[n - 1].strip()
596	        if not text.startswith("|"):
597	            continue
598	        cells = [c.strip() for c in text.strip("|").split("|")]
599	        if cells and cells[0] not in {"", "ID"} and not set(cells[0]) <= set("-: "):
600	            rows.append((n, cells))
601	    return rows
602	
603	
604	def constraints_findings(diff: Diff) -> list[Finding]:
605	    path = "CONSTRAINTS.md"
606	    if path not in diff.added and path not in diff.removed:
607	        return []
608	    still = {
609	        m.group(1) or m.group(2) for _, t in diff.added.get(path, []) if (m := RULE_REF.match(t))
610	    }
611	    gone = {
612	        m.group(1) or m.group(2) for t in diff.removed.get(path, []) if (m := RULE_REF.match(t))
613	    }
614	    found = [Finding("rule-removed", path, 0, f"{rule} removed") for rule in sorted(gone - still)]
615	    lines = Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if Path(path).is_file() else []
616	    start, end = _section(lines, "exceptions")
617	    new_rows = diff.added_lines(path)
618	    found += [
619	        Finding("new-exception", path, n, f"new exception {cells[0]} needs the user's yes")
620	        for n, cells in _table_rows(lines, start, end)
621	        if n in new_rows
622	    ]
623	    return found
624	
625	
626	def load_exceptions(today: date) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
627	    """(rule cell, path glob) for each unexpired row in CONSTRAINTS.md's Exceptions table."""
628	    path = Path("CONSTRAINTS.md")
629	    if not path.is_file():
630	        return []
631	    lines = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
632	    allowed = []
633	    for _, cells in _table_rows(lines, *_section(lines, "exceptions")):
634	        if len(cells) < 6:
635	            continue
636	        expires = re.match(r"\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}", cells[5])
637	        if expires and date.fromisoformat(expires[0]) < today:
638	            continue
639	        allowed.append((cells[1], cells[2].strip("`")))
640	    return allowed
641	
642	
643	def is_excepted(finding: Finding, allowed: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> bool:
644	    rule_id = RULE_IDS.get(finding.rule)
645	    return bool(rule_id) and any(
646	        rule_id in rule and fnmatch.fnmatch(finding.path, glob) for rule, glob in allowed
647	    )
648	
649	
650	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- main
651	def run(
652	    diff: Diff, old_pyproject: Path, python_files: list[str] | None = None, at_exit: bool = False
653	) -> list[Finding]:
654	    found = line_findings(diff) + test_file_findings(diff)
655	    found += task_stub_findings(python_files or [], at_exit)
656	    for path in diff.added:
657	        if is_python(path):
658	            found += ast_findings(path, diff.added_lines(path))
659	    found += config_findings(old_pyproject, diff) + constraints_findings(diff)
660	    allowed = load_exceptions(datetime.now(tz=UTC).date())
661	    return [f for f in found if not is_excepted(f, allowed)]
662	
663	
664	def main(argv: list[str]) -> int:
665	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
666	    parser.add_argument("--diff", type=Path, required=True)
667	    parser.add_argument("--untracked", type=Path, required=True)
668	    parser.add_argument("--old-pyproject", type=Path, required=True)
669	    parser.add_argument(
670	        "--python", type=Path, help="every Python file in the project, one per line"
671	    )
672	    parser.add_argument(
673	        "--exit", action="store_true", help="the milestone exit: no stub may be left"
674	    )
675	    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
676	    diff = parse_diff(args.diff.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"))
677	    add_untracked(diff, args.untracked.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines())
678	    python_files = args.python.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if args.python else []
679	    findings = run(diff, args.old_pyproject, python_files, args.exit)
680	    if not findings:
681	        print("floor_guard: clean", file=sys.stderr)
682	        return 0
683	    print("\n".join(str(f) for f in sorted(findings)))
684	    print(
685	        f"floor_guard: {len(findings)} floor violation(s). Fix the code; a real exception goes "
686	        "through build-setup (Revise) and the Exceptions table in CONSTRAINTS.md.",
687	        file=sys.stderr,
688	    )
689	    return 1
690	
691	
692	if __name__ == "__main__":
693	    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))

1	"""Claim a reserved task half inside the agent's worktree and write its packet to .claude/packet.md.
2	
3	build-phase.md §6.2.
4	Run by the agent as its first action, in its own linked worktree. It turns the reservation into a claim,
5	refuses a stale base (a landing the half needs isn't in HEAD's todo.md), merges the task's tests branch for an
6	implementer, writes `.claude/.task_writes` (the role's share of Writes:), `.claude/.check_pkg` (the package),
7	`.claude/.dt` (this dt.py, for the project's hook scripts), sets `refs/worktree/checks-base` to HEAD, links the
8	project's worktree links, and writes the packet to `.claude/packet.md`.
9	
10	Worktree links: CONSTRAINTS.md may hold one line `**Worktree links:** data/raw, models/cache` naming gitignored
11	inputs (paths relative to the project root, comma-separated, `—` for none). Each that exists in the main checkout
12	and not in the worktree becomes a symlink to the main checkout's copy. `.env` files are never linked.
13	"""
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	
16	import os
17	import re
18	import sys
19	from pathlib import Path
20	
21	from .. import buildstate as bs
22	from .. import dispatch, graph, md
23	from . import context as ctx
24	
25	LINKS_RE = re.compile(r"\*\*Worktree links:\*\*\s*(.*)$")
26	TAIL = 12          # lines of the previous rejection's detail file shown
27	
28	
29	def add_arguments(parser):
30	    parser.add_argument("task", help="task ID, e.g. M1-T03")
31	    parser.add_argument("--role", required=True, choices=bs.ROLES, help="the half this agent builds")
32	
33	
34	def done_condition(task: str, role: str) -> str:
35	    check = "scripts/check.sh collect" if role == "tester" else "scripts/check.sh gate"
36	    what = ("your intent tests lint clean, collect, and are red for the right reason" if role == "tester"
37	            else "the gate passes on your Writes")
38	    return (f"Done when `{check}` passes ({what}); the Stop hook runs it. End with one line: `DONE {task} {role}`. "
39	            f"If you can't finish: `python3 {bs.DT_PY} escalate <TEST-WRONG|NEEDS-DEP> \"<why>\"`, or "
40	            f"`python3 {bs.DT_PY} escalate CONTRACT-CHANGE \"<why>\" --type <the contract symbol>`, "
41	            f"then end with one line: `STOPPED {task} {role}: see dt escalate`.")
42	
43	
44	def worktree_links(repo) -> list[str]:
45	    doc = repo.constraints
46	    for line in doc.lines if doc else []:
47	        m = LINKS_RE.search(line)
48	        if m:
49	            return [p.strip().strip("`").strip("/") for p in md.split_list(m.group(1))
50	                    if p.strip().strip("`") not in ("", "—", "-", "none")]
51	    return []
52	
53	
54	def link(main: Path, wt: Path, rel: str) -> str:
55	    if Path(rel).name.startswith(".env") or rel.startswith(("/", "..")) or "/../" in rel:
56	        return f"{rel}: never linked"
57	    src, dst = main / rel, wt / rel
58	    if not src.exists():
59	        return f"{rel}: not in the main checkout"
60	    if dst.exists() or dst.is_symlink():
61	        return f"{rel}: already present"
62	    dst.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
63	    os.symlink(src, dst)
64	    return f"{rel}: linked"
65	
66	
67	def refuse(msg: str) -> int:
68	    print(f"dt claim: {msg}", file=sys.stderr)
69	    return 1
70	
71	
72	def run(args, repo) -> int:
73	    task_id, role = args.task.upper(), args.role
74	    try:
75	        root = bs.toplevel(repo.root)
76	        state = bs.State(root)
77	        if bs.is_main_checkout(root):
78	            return refuse("run me inside the agent's own worktree, not the main checkout")
79	    except bs.GitError as e:
80	        return refuse(f"needs a git worktree: {e}")
81	    build = dispatch.Build(repo, state)
82	    task = build.by_id.get(task_id)
83	    if task is None:
84	        return refuse(f"{task_id} is not a task of this worktree's tasks/plan.md")
85	    if role not in graph.roles(task):
86	        return refuse(f"{task_id} is Mode {task.mode or '—'}: its halves are {', '.join(graph.roles(task))}")
87	    cid = dispatch.half_id(task_id, role)
88	    claim = state.read("claims", cid)
89	    if claim and bs.real(claim["worktree"]) != bs.real(root):
90	        return refuse(f"{cid} is already claimed in {claim['worktree']}")
91	    if claim is None:
92	        if state.read("reserved", cid) is None:
93	            return refuse(f"{cid} is not reserved: the driver reserves it with `dt ready --reserve`")
94	        # The base must hold every landing the half needs: a stale base fails here, not at merge.
95	        need = ([a for a in graph.ancestors(build.tasks, task_id) if build.by_id[a].kind == "interface"]
96	                if role == "tester" else [d for d in task.depends_on if d in build.by_id])
97	        missing = [d for d in need if d not in build.done]
98	        if missing:
99	            return refuse(f"stale base: HEAD's tasks/todo.md lacks the landing of {', '.join(missing)}; "
100	                          "the worktree must branch from the build branch's head")
101	        if role == "implementer":
102	            branch = bs.tests_branch(task_id)
103	            if not bs.ref_exists(root, f"refs/heads/{branch}"):
104	                return refuse(f"{branch} doesn't exist: the tester half hasn't landed")
105	            merged = bs.git(root, "merge", "--no-edit", "-q", branch, check=False)
106	            if merged.returncode != 0:
107	                bs.git(root, "merge", "--abort", check=False)
108	                return refuse(f"merging {branch} failed: {(merged.stderr or merged.stdout).strip()}")
109	
110	    writes = bs.role_writes(task, role)
111	    claude = root / ".claude"
112	    claude.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
113	    (claude / ".task_writes").write_text("".join(f"{w}\n" for w in writes), encoding="utf-8")
114	    (claude / ".check_pkg").write_text(f"{task.package}\n", encoding="utf-8")
115	    (claude / ".dt").write_text(f"{bs.DT_PY}\n", encoding="utf-8")
116	    links = worktree_links(repo)
117	    state.exclude(["# dt build: files dt claim writes into a worktree"] + [f"/{p}" for p in bs.CLAIM_FILES]
118	                  + [f"/{p}" for p in links])
119	    linked = [link(bs.main_checkout(root), root, p) for p in links]
120	
121	    if claim is None:
122	        bs.git(root, "update-ref", "refs/worktree/checks-base", "HEAD")
123	        claim = {"id": cid, "task": task_id, "role": role, "agent_type": dispatch.agent_type(role),
124	                 "pkg": task.package, "worktree": str(root), "branch": bs.current_branch(root),
125	                 "base": bs.head(root), "time": bs.now(), "finished": False}
126	        state.write("claims", cid, claim)
127	        state.remove("reserved", cid)
128	        state.log("claim", claim=cid, task=task_id, role=role, worktree=str(root), branch=claim.get("branch"),
129	                  base=claim["base"])
130	
131	    p = ctx.packet(repo, task, writes=writes, tester=role == "tester")
132	    head = (f"# Packet: {task_id} {role}\n"
133	            f"You are the {role} for {task_id}. Your edit boundary is your Writes below (`.claude/.task_writes`); "
134	            "the guard blocks everything else.\n\n"
135	            f"{done_condition(task_id, role)}\n\n")
136	    text = head + p["text"]
137	    (claude / "packet.md").write_text(text, encoding="utf-8")
138	
139	    lines = [f"packet: {claude / 'packet.md'} ({text.count(chr(10))} lines; read it in full)",
140	             f"task: {task_id} {task.title} · role {role} · package {task.package or '—'} · kind {task.kind or '—'}",
141	             f"writes: {', '.join(writes) or 'none'}",
142	             f"done: {done_condition(task_id, role)}"]
143	    if p["problems"]:
144	        lines.append("packet problems: " + "; ".join(p["problems"]))
145	    if linked:
146	        lines.append("links: " + "; ".join(linked))
147	    decided = state.read("resolved", task_id) or {}
148	    if decided.get("test_stands") and role == "implementer":
149	        lines.append("the user decided the intent tests stand: make them pass, don't escalate them again"
150	                     + (f" ({decided['note']})" if decided.get("note") else ""))
151	    last = (state.read("rejects", cid) or {}).get("last")
152	    if last:
153	        lines.append(f"previous rejection: {last.get('reject')}"
154	                     + (f" ({last['kind']})" if last.get("kind") else "") + f", details in {last.get('detail_file')}")
155	        try:
156	            detail = Path(last["detail_file"]).read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
157	            lines += [f"  {x}" for x in detail[:TAIL]]
158	        except (OSError, KeyError):
159	            pass
160	    print("\n".join(lines))
161	    return 0
162

full input and output
input
{
  "file_path": "/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-all-at-once-A3-r1/with_skill/project/.claude/worktrees/agent-a8b65a52fd63d455a/scripts/check_size.py"

output
1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""Module and function size checker (Ruff has no rule for either).
3	
4	Usage:
5	    python scripts/check_size.py [PATH ...]      # default: src
6	
7	Limits come from [tool.size_limits] in pyproject.toml:
8	
9	    [tool.size_limits]
10	    max_module_lines = 500      # code lines per file
11	    min_module_lines = 3        # skip __init__.py; catches empty placeholder files
12	    max_function_lines = 60     # code lines per function/method (incl. docstring)
13	
14	"Code lines" = non-blank lines that are not pure comments.
15	Output format mimics Ruff (path:line: CODE message). Exit 1 on any violation.
16	
17	    SIZE001 module too long
18	    SIZE002 module too short
19	    SIZE003 function too long
20	"""
21	
22	from __future__ import annotations
23	
24	import ast
25	import sys
26	import tomllib
27	from pathlib import Path
28	
29	DEFAULTS = {"max_module_lines": 500, "min_module_lines": 3, "max_function_lines": 60}
30	SKIP_DIRS = {".venv", "venv", "node_modules", "__pycache__", ".git", "build", "dist"}
31	
32	
33	def load_limits(pyproject: Path) -> dict[str, int]:
34	    limits = dict(DEFAULTS)
35	    if pyproject.is_file():
36	        data = tomllib.loads(pyproject.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
37	        limits.update(data.get("tool", {}).get("size_limits", {}))
38	    return limits
39	
40	
41	def iter_files(paths: list[str]) -> list[Path]:
42	    files: list[Path] = []
43	    for raw in paths:
44	        p = Path(raw)
45	        if p.is_dir():
46	            files += [f for f in sorted(p.rglob("*.py")) if not SKIP_DIRS & set(f.parts)]
47	        elif p.suffix == ".py" and p.is_file():
48	            files.append(p)
49	    return files
50	
51	
52	def is_code(line: str) -> bool:
53	    s = line.strip()
54	    return bool(s) and not s.startswith("#")
55	
56	
57	def check_file(path: Path, limits: dict[str, int]) -> list[str]:
58	    source = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
59	    lines = source.splitlines()
60	    problems: list[str] = []
61	
62	    n = sum(is_code(line) for line in lines)
63	    if n > limits["max_module_lines"]:
64	        problems.append(
65	            f"{path}:1: SIZE001 module has {n} code lines (max {limits['max_module_lines']})"
66	        )
67	    if path.name != "__init__.py" and n < limits["min_module_lines"]:
68	        problems.append(
69	            f"{path}:1: SIZE002 module has {n} code lines (min {limits['min_module_lines']})"
70	        )
71	
72	    try:
73	        tree = ast.parse(source)
74	    except SyntaxError as exc:
75	        return [*problems, f"{path}:{exc.lineno or 1}: SIZE000 cannot parse: {exc.msg}"]
76	
77	    for node in ast.walk(tree):
78	        if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
79	            start = min([node.lineno, *(d.lineno for d in node.decorator_list)])
80	            body = lines[start - 1 : node.end_lineno]
81	            size = sum(is_code(line) for line in body)
82	            if size > limits["max_function_lines"]:
83	                problems.append(
84	                    f"{path}:{node.lineno}: SIZE003 function `{node.name}` has {size} code lines "
85	                    f"(max {limits['max_function_lines']})"
86	                )
87	    return problems
88	
89	
90	def main(argv: list[str]) -> int:
91	    limits = load_limits(Path("pyproject.toml"))
92	    problems: list[str] = []
93	    for f in iter_files(argv or ["src"]):
94	        problems += check_file(f, limits)
95	    if problems:
96	        print("\n".join(problems))
97	    return 1 if problems else 0
98	
99	
100	if __name__ == "__main__":
101	    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))

wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-all-at-once-A3-r1/with_skill/project/.claude/worktrees/agent-a8b65a52fd63d455a/src/rllab/core/types.py
"""Shared types: action spaces, world specs, steps, episodes, the World and Policy protocols,
the held-out seed sets and outputs (contract: core).

`MethodConfig` and `RunConfig` are defined here too, because `runlog` (which may import only
`core`) stores them in run records and checkpoints; their contract names are
`methods.MethodConfig` and `train.RunConfig`, which re-export them.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Literal, Protocol

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

Direction = Literal["higher", "lower"]
Settings = Mapping[str, int | float | str | bool]
Array = npt.NDArray[Any]

EVAL_SEEDS: tuple[int, ...] = tuple(range(1_000_000, 1_000_020))
"""The 20 in-training evaluation seeds, disjoint from training seeds and FINAL_SEEDS."""

FINAL_SEEDS: tuple[int, ...] = tuple(range(2_000_000, 2_000_100))
"""The 100 final evaluation seeds, shared by every run, baseline and reference."""


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Discrete:
    """Actions are ints in [0, n)."""

    n: int


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Box:
    """Actions are `dims` floats in [low, high]."""

    dims: int
    low: float
    high: float


ActionSpace = Discrete | Box


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TeamSpec:
    """A team: indices into the world's agent axis, and how its score is read."""

    name: str
    agents: tuple[int, ...]
    score_name: str
    direction: Direction


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MeasureSpec:
    """An extra per-episode measure a world reports for one team."""

    name: str
    team: str
    direction: Direction


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class WorldSpec:
    """Everything known about a world without running an episode (WORLD-04)."""

    world: str
    variant: str
    agents: tuple[str, ...]
    teams: tuple[TeamSpec, ...]
    obs_len: int
    action: ActionSpace
    max_steps: int
    measures: tuple[MeasureSpec, ...]
    sight: float | None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Step:
    """One world step: float32 obs (n_agents x obs_len), float32 rewards, bool active mask."""

    obs: Array
    rewards: Array
    active: Array
    done: bool
    truncated: bool


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EpisodeResult:
    """Scores and team rewards keyed by team; measures keyed by every MeasureSpec name."""

    seed: int
    length: int
    scores: Mapping[str, float]
    team_rewards: Mapping[str, float]
    measures: Mapping[str, float]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Drawable:
    """One object on screen; `agent` is set for agents."""

    kind: str
    x: float
    y: float
    size: float
    team: str | None
    agent: int | None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DrawState:
    """Every object on screen at one step (WORLD-06)."""

    width: float
    height: float
    objects: tuple[Drawable, ...]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Frame:
    """One traced step: running team scores and the latest per-agent rewards."""

    step: int
    draw: DrawState
    scores: Mapping[str, float]
    rewards: Array


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Episode:
    """A played episode; `frames` holds `result.length + 1` entries when traced, else None."""

    result: EpisodeResult
    frames: tuple[Frame, ...] | None
    hit_step_limit: bool


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Output:
    """A written chart, video or write-up, and the runs, checkpoints and seeds behind it."""

    path: Path
    kind: Literal["chart", "video", "writeup"]
    made_from: tuple[str, ...]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MethodConfig:
    """A learning method and its full settings (contract: methods.MethodConfig)."""

    method: Literal["dqn", "ppo"]
    settings: Settings


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RunConfig:
    """One training run's settings (contract: train.RunConfig)."""

    name: str
    world: str
    variant: str
    methods: Mapping[str, MethodConfig]
    seed: int
    budget: int
    check_settings: Mapping[str, Settings]
    device: str = "cpu"


class ActionError(ValueError):
    """An action outside the world's action space; never clipped."""

    def __init__(self, agent: str, value: object) -> None:
        super().__init__(f"action {value!r} of agent {agent!r} is outside the action space")
        self.agent = agent
        self.value = value


class World(Protocol):
    """A multi-agent world: seeded reset, one action row per agent, per-team scores."""

    spec: WorldSpec

    def reset(self, seed: int) -> Step: ...

    def step(self, actions: Array) -> Step: ...

    def result(self) -> EpisodeResult: ...

    def draw(self) -> DrawState: ...


class Policy(Protocol):
    """Acts for the k agents of one team, deterministically for a given obs and rng state."""

    def act(self, obs: Array, rng: np.random.Generator) -> Array: ...
full input and output

wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-all-at-once-A3-r1/with_skill/project/.claude/worktrees/agent-a8b65a52fd63d455a/src/rllab/worlds/checks.py
"""Check worlds with hand-derived best actions (PL-01 steps 4-5, AD-09)."""

from __future__ import annotations

from rllab.core.types import Array, DrawState, EpisodeResult, Step, WorldSpec


class CheckDiscrete:
    """The 6-state two-exit corridor, a `worlds.CheckWorld` with `Discrete(2)` actions."""

    spec: WorldSpec

"""Types of the methods component (contract: methods).

`MethodConfig` lives in `core.types` so `runlog` can store it; it is re-exported here.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

import numpy as np

from rllab.core.types import Array, MethodConfig, Policy

__all__ = ["Learner", "MethodConfig", "NonFiniteError", "SpecMismatchError", "Transition"]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Transition:
    """One world step seen by one team: rows are the team's agents in TeamSpec.agents order."""

    obs: Array
    actions: Array
    rewards: Array
    next_obs: Array
    done: bool
    truncated: bool
    active: Array


class NonFiniteError(FloatingPointError):
    """A loss or parameter became NaN or infinite; raised before any parameter changes."""

    def __init__(self, value: float) -> None:
        super().__init__(f"non-finite value {value!r}")
        self.value = value


class SpecMismatchError(ValueError):
    """A checkpoint's world spec differs from the world's: field -> (checkpoint, world)."""

    def __init__(self, fields: Mapping[str, tuple[object, object]]) -> None:
        names = ", ".join(f"{k} ({a!r} != {b!r})" for k, (a, b) in fields.items())
        super().__init__(f"checkpoint spec differs from the world in: {names}")
        self.fields = dict(fields)


class Learner(Protocol):
    """A hand-written learner for one team (CHECK-01)."""

    def act(self, obs: Array, rng: np.random.Generator) -> Array:
        """Exploring actions for the team's agents."""
        ...

    def observe(self, t: Transition) -> None:
        """Learn from one transition, updating on the learner's own schedule."""
        ...

    def policy(self) -> Policy:
        """A frozen, deterministic copy: greedy (DQN) or the mean action (PPO)."""
        ...

    def state_dict(self) -> dict[str, object]:
        """Everything a checkpoint needs to rebuild the policy."""
        ...
